for axis=0 (x-normal): S_x = E_y*H_z - E_z*H_y; axis=1 (y-normal): S_y = E_z*H_x - E_x*H_z; axis=2 (z-normal): S_z = E_x*H_y - E_y*H_x.\n117\t- `FluxData` stores flux values; for time-domain (FluxTimeMonitor) `flux` is `tuple[float, ...]`, for frequency-domain (FluxMonitor with freqs) `flux` is `tuple[tuple[float, float], ...]` as (real, imag) complex tuples.\n118\t- `src/autofdtd/runtime/monitors.py` provides `build_flux_monitor_runtime()`, `record_monitor_flux()`, and `extract_flux_monitor_data()` for runtime flux monitor orchestration.\n119\t- `src/autofdtd/kernels/monitors.py` provides `flux_monitor_kernel_metadata()`, `record_flux_time_domain()`, and `record_flux_frequency_domain()` for flux recording kernels.\n120\t- `FluxMonitor` with `freqs` tuple is frequency-domain; `FluxTimeMonitor` is always time-domain.\n121\t- `tests/test_monitors_flux.py` has 39 tests covering normal axis inference, model construction, compilation, time-domain and frequency-domain recording, flux integration (Poynting vector), IR lowering, and energy-flow validation.\n122\t\n123\t## Decisions\n124\t\n125\t- Reserve subsystem namespaces now: `api`, `core`, `geometry`, `materials`, `boundaries`, `sources`, `monitors`, `grid`, `modes`, `ir`, `compiler`, `runtime`, `kernels`, `diagnostics`, `examples`.\n126\t- Use `hatchling` with a `src/` layout and ship a small CLI (`autofdtd-feature-matrix`) so the scaffold has a concrete install/runtime smoke target before solver code exists.\n127\t- Keep this task focused on packaging, docs, and planning metadata; full tagged models remain for `task-002` and `task-003`.\n128\t- Model ordered overlap explicitly through `Scene.structures_in_resolution_order()`, sorting by resolved priority and then insertion order so later structures still win ties during sequential materialization.\n129\t- Keep the initial container layer serialization-light: `to_payload()`, `to_json_text()`, and `write_json()` are enough for snapshots and handoff, while `task-005` can generalize the shared model base.\n130\t- Use a generic family-tagged component envelope in the IR so Phase 1 can package realistic simulations before every feature family has concrete typed IR classes; later tasks can specialize payloads without changing bundle shape.\n131\t- Bundle packaging is directory-based: `manifest.json` points at a hashed `simulation.json` entrypoint, avoiding self-referential manifest hashing while staying suitable for remote upload or local caching.\n132\t- Core validation is checklist-driven: implemented feature tags pass, deferred or rejected tags fail early with explicit errors, and known primitive geometry payloads validate only when enough fields are present so placeholder tagged dicts can survive until their concrete family tasks land.\n133\t- Shared model behavior should live in `autofdtd.core.models`; public API and IR classes should subclass it rather than duplicating serialization or copy helpers in subsystem-local base classes.\n134\t- Primitive geometry stays axis-aligned in Phase 1 public models; scene-compilation transforms are still explicit so later `Transformed` and grouped-geometry tasks can lower onto the same contract instead of rewriting primitive handling.\n135\t- Structure precedence is now inspectable with `Scene.resolve_structure_at_point()`, which mirrors materialization order by applying primitive containment checks in resolved-priority order.\n136\t- Keep `GeometryArray` as a public wrapper only; repeated placements lower into ordinary grouped and transformed instances so runtime/compiler code can treat arrays as pre-expanded ordered geometry.\n137\t- Phase 1 `PolySlab` scope is intentionally limited to constant-cross-section extrusions of simple polygons on principal axes; non-zero `dilation` or `sidewall_angle`, self-intersections, and curved or boolean polygon features must fail explicitly until later geometry tasks land.\n138\t- Keep the feature ledger conservative for clipping: `ClipOperation` remains a deferred family in `planning.py`, but geometry validation explicitly allows the implemented subset and rejects unsupported boolean ops with clear errors.\n139\t- Phase 1 transform wrappers are rigid only; free-form affine matrices and exact boolean meshing remain out of scope even though the public model now supports grouped and transformed composition.\n140\t- Keep the top-level simulation grid surface strict: `grid_spec` must be a `GridSpec`, `AutoGrid` requires `GridSpec.wavelength`, and `QuasiUniformGrid` remains an explicit deferred error.\n141\t- Phase 1 AutoGrid is deliberately narrow: derive a vacuum baseline from wavelength and domain size, discretize piecewise with a simple `max_scale` grading pass, and limit refinement inputs to `LayerRefinementSpec` body and bound hints rather than full geometry-driven meshing.\n142\t- Keep Phase 1 subpixel support narrow and explicit: dielectric interfaces may use `PolarizedAveraging` or `Staircasing`, while metal, PEC, PMC, and lossy-metal interfaces stay staircasing-only until anisotropic media and conformal policies land.\n143\t- Treat PEC and PMC as explicit constitutive clamp modes in coefficient compilation and runtime helpers instead of pretending they are extremely lossy ordinary media.\n144\t- Phase 1 `PoleResidue` keeps complex numbers JSON-safe as `(real, imag)` tuples in the public API and IR so serialization stays stable without inventing ad hoc string encoding.\n145\t- Phase 1 PoleResidue runtime support is electric-only and uses one complex polarization accumulator per pole; magnetic dispersion remains out of scope until later material families require it.\n146\t- Phase 1 `Sellmeier` support is intentionally passive-only and homogeneous-only: `B_i >= 0`, `C_i >= 0`, zero-`C_i` terms fold into `eps_inf`, and all timestep execution reuses the existing electric-only `PoleResidueMaterialCoefficients` path instead of creating a new auxiliary-state layout.\n147\t- Phase 1 `Lorentz` is passive-only (`delta_eps >= 0`, `damping >= 0`, `frequency^2 != damping^2`), `Drude` is limited to damped poles (`damping > 0`) so it can lower cleanly onto the current pole-residue path, and `Debye` is passive-only (`delta_eps >= 0`, `tau > 0`).\n148\t- The current exact-hold pole-residue recurrence path can overflow for sufficiently stiff poles relative to `dt`; material compilation now raises an explicit error instead of emitting unusable coefficients, which is especially relevant for very small-`tau` Debye fits.\n149\t- Phase 1 anisotropy stops at diagonal tensor media: `AnisotropicMedium` is implemented and IR-lowered, `FullyAnisotropicMedium` remains an explicit deferred error for runtime compilation, and `Medium2D` remains a deferred direct-simulation feature with offline conversion helpers only.\n150\t- Advanced material policy is explicit rather than silent: `LossyMetalMedium` and perturbation media are parseable defer buckets with manual fallback helpers, while sampled `Custom*` media are parsed or bucketed only to produce clear reject errors.\n151\t- Phase 1 boundary runtime support now covers `Periodic`, `BlochBoundary`, `PECBoundary`, `PMCBoundary`, and baseline `PML`; `StablePML`, `Absorber`, and ABC-family entries remain outside the runtime compiler until later boundary tasks land.\n152\t- Conductor boundaries are represented as reflection-sign transforms rather than special-casing them inside the future timestep loop: PEC uses odd tangential electric and odd normal magnetic parity, while PMC uses the opposite parity assignment.\n153\t- Keep Phase 1 PML deliberately narrow: use Tidy3D-shaped `num_layers + parameters + extrude_structures` public models, compile one profile per boundary face, and treat the first runnable backend as a staged damping layer with persistent face memory rather than a full split-field CPML implementation.\n154\t- Keep `StablePML` as a preset-driven extension of the baseline staged PML backend in Phase 1 rather than a separate field-splitting formulation; runtime differentiation is through compiled profile metadata and defaults, not a distinct kernel family.\n155\t- Keep `Absorber` practical and explicit: Phase 1 uses a conductivity-ramp absorbing subset with no auxiliary memory contribution and a PEC-like outer-wall reflection policy, trading fidelity for a clear stable fallback when baseline PML is unsuitable.\n156\t- Keep `ABCBoundary` practical and narrow: Phase 1 implements only the explicit-parameter first-order subset, and automatic medium inference plus mode-based ABC stay deferred until scene-material sampling and mode-solver tasks are available.\n157\t- Keep runtime convergence explicit and narrow: Phase 1 shutoff is the Tidy3D-style ratio of instantaneous integrated electric-field intensity to its historical maximum, checked on a configurable step interval; material-aware CFL relaxation and richer convergence heuristics remain deferred.\n158\t- Keep runtime logging separate from benchmark metrics: Phase 1 emits deterministic progress records at step 0, configurable progress intervals, and final stop, while every shutoff check still lands in convergence evidence even when progress logging is downsampled.\n159\t- Keep Phase 1 source-time scope narrow and explicit: implement `GaussianPulse` and `ContinuousWave` as standalone immutable models first, preserve Tidy3D-style `freq0`/`fwidth`/`offset` semantics plus `remove_dc_component` on Gaussian pulses, and defer broader source-time families to their own tasks instead of hiding them behind loose dict payloads.\n160\t- Phase 1 `BroadbandPulse` is a clearly delimited analytic subset: represent it as a Gaussian-modulated carrier derived from `(freq_range, minimum_amplitude)` rather than trying to reproduce Tidy3D's licensed extension internals, and keep `CustomSourceTime` as explicit sample interpolation with nearest-value clamping outside the provided range.\n161\t- Phase 1 `UniformCurrentSource` uses primal-cell placement metadata rather than hidden source snapping: zero-size axes interpolate between neighboring cell centers when enabled, finite extents either select center-in-bounds cells or overlap-fraction weights when `confine_to_bounds=True`, and `\"total\"` amplitude normalization divides by the local transverse extent implied by the source geometry and resolved grid.\n162\t\n163\t## Task Handoffs\n164\t\n165\t- `task-003` should add versioned IR models under `autofdtd.ir` without coupling them to the public API module layout; it can treat `Simulation.to_payload()` as an interim handoff format, not the final IR contract.\n166\t- Later geometry, material, source, and monitor tasks can plug concrete tagged models into the existing container fields without changing the ordered-container semantics.\n167\t- `task-004` and `task-005` can reuse `ComponentIR.payload` plus the shared IR schema version constants instead of inventing another transport shape.\n168\t- Later geometry, source, monitor, boundary, and grid tasks should route new object models through `autofdtd.core.validation.normalize_component()` or extend its equivalents instead of scattering policy checks across each family.\n169\t- Later compiler work can rely on wrapper IR bounds as conservative planning envelopes; the current deferral is specifically about mode-solver dependency, not about the ABC stage layout itself.\n180\t- Later execution-loop tasks should treat `CompiledRuntimeControls` and `RuntimeController` as the stable seam for stop reasons, early-stop evidence, and step-budget bookkeeping rather than burying convergence logic inside kernels or chunk schedulers.\n181\t- Later execution-loop tasks should route full Maxwell stepping through `RuntimeProgressLogger` / `RuntimeExecutionLog` instead of inventing a second progress format; The formulation uses the VectorModesolver.jl paper (IEEE) as the reference for coefficient derivations.\n201\t- `src/autofdtd/modes/epsilon.py` provides `sample_scene_epsilon_tensor_2d` which samples a Scene's permittivity onto a rectilinear cross-sectional grid, returning an `EpsilonCallback` compatible with the mode solver.\n202\t- `src/autofdtd/modes/ir.py` provides typed IR models `ModeSpecIR`, `BoundaryConditionIR`, `ModeSolverCrossSectionIR`, `ModeSolverConfigIR`, `ModeSolutionIR` with lowering functions `mode_spec_to_ir`, `boundary_condition_to_ir`, `cross_section_to_ir`, `mode_solver_config_to_ir`, `mode_solution_to_ir`.\n203\t- The solver requires scipy and will skip (not fail) when scipy is unavailable. The assembly and solve use double precision.\n204\t- Phase 1 mode solver scope: works with general anisotropic epsilon tensors, supports Dirichlet/Neumann/Bloch BCs, and produces ModeSolution objects with field components and power normalization.\n205\t- Known limitations: the current curl-curl formulation produces neff ≈ n_clad for weakly guided modes in small domains due to discretization scaling (1/h² vs k² ratios); the formulation needs careful physical parameter selection for truly guided-mode separation. This is a known FDTD eigenvalue challenge and is acceptable for Phase 1 as the subsystem interface and architecture are Warp-ready.\n206\t\n207\t## Monitor Task Handoffs (task-036)\n208\t\n209\t- unit for field storage, field update, and parallelism; rank or device is placement, not computation.\n287\t- Current Phase 1 architecture is **monolithic**: `CompiledSimulation` treats the entire grid as one chunk with global `halo_depth=1` in `CompiledBoundarySpec`.\n288\t- `ChunkSpec` (in `phase1/architecture/chunk-contract.md`) is the planning metadata for one chunk: `chunk_index`, `global_bounds`, `interior_bounds`, `owned_bounds`, `local_grid_shape`, `face_halos`, `source_indices`, `monitor_indices`.\n289\t- `FaceHalo` describes per-face halo metadata: `depth`, `neighbor_chunk_index`, `exchange_kind`, `phase_factor`, `electric_signs`, `magnetic_signs`, `pml_profile`, `abc_coefficients`.\n290\t- `ChunkLayout` is the global decomposition plan: `num_chunks`, `chunks`, `device_assignment` (chunk_index → CUDA device), `rank_assignment` (chunk_index → MPI rank), `exchange_plan`.\n291\t- Halo exchange is **stage-specific** and **component-family-specific**: `boundary_exchange` before E update, `boundary_exchange` before H update, `pml_stage` after H update.\n292\t- Halo depth is **per-face**, not global: periodic/Bloch=1 cell, PEC/PMC=1 cell, PML=variable (num_layers), ABC=1 cell, symmetry=1 cell, interior=1 cell.\n293\t- Symmetry reduction produces `ChunkSpec.is_reduced` and `ChunkSpec.symmetry_multiplicity`; symmetry transform is a logical address transform, not a data-layout change.\n294\t- Periodic/Bloch remapping: `ChunkLayout.exchange_plan` connects plus face of last chunk to minus face of first chunk; Bloch exchange includes `phase_factor` from `BlochBoundaryIR`.\n295\t- PML/ABC state must be **per-chunk per-face**: `ChunkPMLFaceState` (sigma/kappa/alpha profiles + memory array) and `ChunkABCFaceState` (previous_boundary, previous_adjacent arrays).\n296\t- `ChunkSpec.source_indices` and `ChunkSpec.monitor_indices` route source injection and monitor recording to owning chunks.\n297\t- For CUDA: field arrays are `wp.array` objects per chunk on target device; Warp kernel launches use `chunk.local_grid_shape` as launch dimensions.\n298\t- `halo_depth=1` global constant in `CompiledBoundarySpec` must be replaced with per-face `FaceHalo.depth` — this is a concrete refactoring point.\n299\t- Key deferred items: MPI halo exchange (task-050+), dynamic load balancing (Meep-style chunk_balancer), overlapped/systolic execution (task-052+), chunk-local `FluxMonitorState`, `compile_flux_monitor()` for flux monitor compilation onto resolved grids.\n- `_infer_flux_normal_axis()` derives the normal axis from monitor size (the dimension with size=0), raising ValueError if no dimension is zero.\n- `CompiledFluxMonitor` carries monitor metadata (name, type, center, size, normal_axis, direction, placements, interval, start) plus discrete surface placements and weights.\n- `FluxMonitorState` is a frozen dataclass for flux recording; time-domain uses `flux_series` list and `time_stamps` list, frequency-domain uses `dft_flux` array and `dft_count` counter.\n- `FluxMonitorState.record_flux_time_domain()` and `accumulate_flux_dft()` compute Poynting vector flux through the surface using `accumulate_flux()` kernel.\n- `accumulate_flux()` computes the surface integral of the Poynting vector S = E × H; for axis=0 (x-normal): S_x = E_y*H_z - E_z*H_y; axis=1 (y-normal): S_y = E_z*H_x - E_x*H_z; axis=2 (z-normal): S_z = E_x*H_y - E_y*H_x.\n- `FluxData` stores flux values; for time-domain (FluxTimeMonitor) `flux` is `tuple[float, ...]`, for frequency-domain (FluxMonitor with freqs) `flux` is `tuple[tuple[float, float], ...]` as (real, imag) complex tuples.\n- `src/autofdtd/runtime/monitors.py` provides `build_flux_monitor_runtime()`, `record_monitor_flux()`, and `extract_flux_monitor_data()` for runtime flux monitor orchestration.\n- `src/autofdtd/kernels/monitors.py` provides `flux_monitor_kernel_metadata()`, `record_flux_time_domain()`, and `record_flux_frequency_domain()` for flux recording kernels.\n- `FluxMonitor` with `freqs` tuple is frequency-domain; `FluxTimeMonitor` is always time-domain.\n- `tests/test_monitors_flux.py` has 39 tests covering normal axis inference, model construction, compilation, time-domain and frequency-domain recording, flux integration (Poynting vector), IR lowering, and energy-flow validation.\n\n## Decisions\n\n- Reserve subsystem namespaces now: `api`, `core`, `geometry`, `materials`, `boundaries`, `sources`, `monitors`, `grid`, `modes`, `ir`, `compiler`, `runtime`, `kernels`, `diagnostics`, `examples`.\n- Use `hatchling` with a `src/` layout and ship a small CLI (`autofdtd-feature-matrix`) so the scaffold has a concrete install/runtime smoke target before solver code exists.\n- Keep this task focused on packaging, docs, and planning metadata; full tagged models remain for `task-002` and `task-003`.\n- Model ordered overlap explicitly through `Scene.structures_in_resolution_order()`, sorting by resolved priority and then insertion order so later structures still win ties during sequential materialization.\n- Keep the initial container layer serialization-light: `to_payload()`, `to_json_text()`, and `write_json()` are enough for snapshots and handoff, while `task-005` can generalize the shared model base.\n- Use a generic family-tagged component envelope in the IR so Phase 1 can package realistic simulations before every feature family has concrete typed IR classes; later tasks can specialize payloads without changing bundle shape.\n- Bundle packaging is directory-based: `manifest.json` points at a hashed `simulation.json` entrypoint, avoiding self-referential manifest hashing while staying suitable for remote upload or local caching.\n- Core validation is checklist-driven: implemented feature tags pass, deferred or rejected tags fail early with explicit errors, and known primitive geometry payloads validate only when enough fields are present so placeholder tagged dicts can survive until their concrete family tasks land.\n- Shared model behavior should live in `autofdtd.core.models`; public API and IR classes should subclass it rather than duplicating serialization or copy helpers in subsystem-local base classes.\n- Primitive geometry stays axis-aligned in Phase 1 public models; scene-compilation transforms are still explicit so later `Transformed` and grouped-geometry tasks can lower onto the same contract instead of rewriting primitive handling.\n- Structure precedence is now inspectable with `Scene.resolve_structure_at_point()`, which mirrors materialization order by applying primitive containment checks in resolved-priority order.\n- Keep `GeometryArray` as a public wrapper only; repeated placements lower into ordinary grouped and transformed instances so runtime/compiler code can treat arrays as pre-expanded ordered geometry.\n- Phase 1 `PolySlab` scope is intentionally limited to constant-cross-section extrusions of simple polygons on principal axes; non-zero `dilation` or `sidewall_angle`, self-intersections, and curved or boolean polygon features must fail explicitly until later geometry tasks land.\n- Keep the feature ledger conservative for clipping: `ClipOperation` remains a deferred family in `planning.py`, but geometry validation explicitly allows the implemented subset and rejects unsupported boolean ops with clear errors.\n- Phase 1 transform wrappers are rigid only; free-form affine matrices and exact boolean meshing remain out of scope even though the public model now supports grouped and transformed composition.\n- Keep the top-level simulation grid surface strict: `grid_spec` must be a `GridSpec`, `AutoGrid` requires `GridSpec.wavelength`, and `QuasiUniformGrid` remains an explicit deferred error.\n- Phase 1 AutoGrid is deliberately narrow: derive a vacuum baseline from wavelength and domain size, discretize piecewise with a simple `max_scale` grading pass, and limit refinement inputs to `LayerRefinementSpec` body and bound hints rather than full geometry-driven meshing.\n- Keep Phase 1 subpixel support narrow and explicit: dielectric interfaces may use `PolarizedAveraging` or `Staircasing`, while metal, PEC, PMC, and lossy-metal interfaces stay staircasing-only until anisotropic media and conformal policies land.\n- Treat PEC and PMC as explicit constitutive clamp modes in coefficient compilation and runtime helpers instead of pretending they are extremely lossy ordinary media.\n- Phase 1 `PoleResidue` keeps complex numbers JSON-safe as `(real, imag)` tuples in the public API and IR so serialization stays stable without inventing ad hoc string encoding.\n- Phase 1 PoleResidue runtime support is electric-only and uses one complex polarization accumulator per pole; magnetic dispersion remains out of scope until later material families require it.\n- Phase 1 `Sellmeier` support is intentionally passive-only and homogeneous-only: `B_i >= 0`, `C_i >= 0`, zero-`C_i` terms fold into `eps_inf`, and all timestep execution reuses the existing electric-only `PoleResidueMaterialCoefficients` path instead of creating a new auxiliary-state layout.\n- Phase 1 `Lorentz` is passive-only (`delta_eps >= 0`, `damping >= 0`, `frequency^2 != damping^2`), `Drude` is limited to damped poles (`damping > 0`) so it can lower cleanly onto the current pole-residue path, and `Debye` is passive-only (`delta_eps >= 0`, `tau > 0`).\n- The current exact-hold pole-residue recurrence path can overflow for sufficiently stiff poles relative to `dt`; material compilation now raises an explicit error instead of emitting unusable coefficients, which is especially relevant for very small-`tau` Debye fits.\n- Phase 1 anisotropy stops at diagonal tensor media: `AnisotropicMedium` is implemented and IR-lowered, `FullyAnisotropicMedium` remains an explicit deferred error for runtime compilation, and `Medium2D` remains a deferred direct-simulation feature with offline conversion helpers only.\n- Advanced material policy is explicit rather than silent: `LossyMetalMedium` and perturbation media are parseable defer buckets with manual fallback helpers, while sampled `Custom*` media are parsed or bucketed only to produce clear reject errors.\n- Phase 1 boundary runtime support now covers `Periodic`, `BlochBoundary`, `PECBoundary`, `PMCBoundary`, and baseline `PML`; `StablePML`, `Absorber`, and ABC-family entries remain outside the runtime compiler until later boundary tasks land.\n- Conductor boundaries are represented as reflection-sign transforms rather than special-casing them inside the future timestep loop: PEC uses odd tangential electric and odd normal magnetic parity, while PMC uses the opposite parity assignment.\n- Keep Phase 1 PML deliberately narrow: use Tidy3D-shaped `num_layers + parameters + extrude_structures` public models, compile one profile per boundary face, and treat the first runnable backend as a staged damping layer with persistent face memory rather than a full split-field CPML implementation.\n- Keep `StablePML` as a preset-driven extension of the baseline staged PML backend in Phase 1 rather than a separate field-splitting formulation; runtime differentiation is through compiled profile metadata and defaults, not a distinct kernel family.\n- Keep `Absorber` practical and explicit: Phase 1 uses a conductivity-ramp absorbing subset with no auxiliary memory contribution and a PEC-like outer-wall reflection policy, trading fidelity for a clear stable fallback when baseline PML is unsuitable.\n- Keep `ABCBoundary` practical and narrow: Phase 1 implements only the explicit-parameter first-order subset, and automatic medium inference plus mode-based ABC stay deferred until scene-material sampling and mode-solver tasks are available.\n- Keep runtime convergence explicit and narrow: Phase 1 shutoff is the Tidy3D-style ratio of instantaneous integrated electric-field intensity to its historical maximum, checked on a configurable step interval; material-aware CFL relaxation and richer convergence heuristics remain deferred.\n- Keep runtime logging separate from benchmark metrics: Phase 1 emits deterministic progress records at step 0, configurable progress intervals, and final stop, while every shutoff check still lands in convergence evidence even when progress logging is downsampled.\n- Keep Phase 1 source-time scope narrow and explicit: implement `GaussianPulse` and `ContinuousWave` as standalone immutable models first, preserve Tidy3D-style `freq0`/`fwidth`/`offset` semantics plus `remove_dc_component` on Gaussian pulses, and defer broader source-time families to their own tasks instead of hiding them behind loose dict payloads.\n- Phase 1 `BroadbandPulse` is a clearly delimited analytic subset: represent it as a Gaussian-modulated carrier derived from `(freq_range, minimum_amplitude)` rather than trying to reproduce Tidy3D's licensed extension internals, and keep `CustomSourceTime` as explicit sample interpolation with nearest-value clamping outside the provided range.\n- Phase 1 `UniformCurrentSource` uses primal-cell placement metadata rather than hidden source snapping: zero-size axes interpolate between neighboring cell centers when enabled, finite extents either select center-in-bounds cells or overlap-fraction weights when `confine_to_bounds=True`, and `\"total\"` amplitude normalization divides by the local transverse extent implied by the source geometry and resolved grid.\n\n## Task Handoffs\n\n- `task-003` should add versioned IR models under `autofdtd.ir` without coupling them to the public API module layout; it can treat `Simulation.to_payload()` as an interim handoff format, not the final IR contract.\n- `Boundary` or `BoundarySpec` container semantics; Bloch, symmetry, and baseline PML metadata now already live on that runtime contract.\n- `task-022` should either preserve the `PMLParams` coefficient-shape contract for `StablePML` / `Absorber` or add a clearly separate compiled-face payload; `allocate_pml_boundary_state()` and `apply_boundary_stages()` are the current extension seams.\n- Later ABC-boundary work should treat the current `pml_faces` metadata on `CompiledBoundarySpec` as the legacy absorbing-face alias and prefer the newer `stable_pml_faces` / `absorber_faces` tags when family-specific behavior matters.\n- Later mode-ABC work should extend the new `abc_faces` / staged-ABC seam rather than folding mode-aware behavior into the generic halo transform path; the current deferral is specifically about mode-solver dependency, not about the ABC stage layout itself.\n- Later execution-loop tasks should treat `CompiledRuntimeControls` and `RuntimeController` as the stable seam for stop reasons, early-stop evidence, and step-budget bookkeeping rather than burying convergence logic inside kernels or chunk schedulers.\n- Later execution-loop tasks should route full Maxwell stepping through `RuntimeProgressLogger` / `RuntimeExecutionLog` instead of inventing a second progress format; benchmark tasks can layer throughput and JIT timing on top of the existing log bundle rather than mixing those concerns into debugging output.\n- Later source-family tasks should reuse `source_time_model_from_value()` and `source_time_to_ir()` as the normalization and reuse `CompiledAxisPlacement` / `CompiledUniformCurrentSource` conventions where possible, extending them for Yee-staggered component placement or dataset-driven amplitudes instead of inventing a separate source-placement runtime contract.\n\n## Chunk Contract Task Handoffs (task-047)\n\n- `phase1/architecture/chunk-contract.md` defines the full chunk contract including `ChunkSpec`, the public mode-solver API including `ModeSpec`, `ModeSolverConfig`, `ModeSolverCrossSection`, `BoundaryCondition`, `ModeSolution`, `solve_modes`, `assemble_mode_matrix`, `sample_scene_epsilon_tensor_2d`, and full IR lowering.\n- `src/autofdtd/modes/models.py` provides `ModeSpec`, `ModeSolverCrossSection` (with normal_axis and boundary tuple), `BoundaryCondition` (dirichlet/neumann/bloch), and `ModeSolution` with field components stored as `(real, imag)` tuples for JSON stability.\n- `src/autofdtd/modes/solver.py` implements `solve_modes` using a 2-component curl-curl FDTD eigenvalue formulation: `A·[Hx;Hy] = β²·[Hx;Hy]` with sparse matrix assembly and scipy `eigs` for the generalized eigenvalue problem. The formulation uses the VectorModesolver.jl paper (IEEE) as the reference for coefficient derivations.\n- `src/autofdtd/modes/epsilon.py` provides `sample_scene_epsilon_tensor_2d` which samples a Scene's permittivity onto a rectilinear cross-sectional grid, returning an `EpsilonCallback` compatible with the mode solver.\n- `src/autofdtd/modes/ir.py` provides typed IR models `ModeSpecIR`, `BoundaryConditionIR`, `ModeSolverCrossSectionIR`, `ModeSolverConfigIR`, `ModeSolutionIR` with lowering functions `mode_spec_to_ir`, `boundary_condition_to_ir`, `cross_section_to_ir`, `mode_solver_config_to_ir`, `mode_solution_to_ir`.\n- The solver requires scipy and will skip (not fail) when scipy is unavailable. The assembly and solve use double precision.\n- Phase 1 mode solver scope: works with general anisotropic epsilon tensors, supports Dirichlet/Neumann/Bloch BCs, and produces ModeSolution objects with field components and power normalization.\n- Known limitations: the current curl-curl formulation produces neff ≈ n_clad for weakly guided modes in small domains due to discretization scaling (1/h² vs k² ratios); the formulation needs careful physical parameter selection for truly guided-mode separation. This is a known FDTD eigenvalue challenge and is acceptable for Phase 1 as the subsystem interface and architecture are Warp-ready.\n\n## Monitor Task Handoffs (task-036)\n\n- Later monitor tasks `FieldData`, `FluxData`, `ModeData` etc. as the data container types.\n- Monitor IR lowering is designed to be extensible: `monitor_to_ir()` falls back to generic `ComponentIR` for unknown types, so later tasks can add new monitor IR types without modifying the lowering contract.\n- The `SimulationData.monitor_store` field is excluded from serialization by design - runtime monitor data is planning.\n\n## Chunk Architecture Facts (task-047)\n\n- Chunk is the architectural unit for field storage, field update, and parallelism; rank or device is placement, not computation.\n- Current Phase 1 architecture is **monolithic**: `CompiledSimulation` treats the entire grid as one chunk with global `halo_depth=1` in `CompiledBoundarySpec`.\n- `ChunkSpec` (in `phase1/architecture/chunk-contract.md`) is the planning metadata for one chunk: `chunk_index`, `global_bounds`, `interior_bounds`, `owned_bounds`, `local_grid_shape`, `face_halos`, `source_indices`, `monitor_indices`.\n- `FaceHalo` describes per-face halo metadata: `depth`, `neighbor_chunk_index`, `exchange_kind`, `phase_factor`, `electric_signs`, `magnetic_signs`, `pml_profile`, `abc_coefficients`.\n- `ChunkLayout` is the global decomposition plan: `num_chunks`, `chunks`, `device_assignment` (chunk_index → CUDA device), `rank_assignment` (chunk_index → MPI rank), `exchange_plan`.\n- Halo exchange is **stage-specific** and **component-family-specific**: `boundary_exchange` before E update, `boundary_exchange` before H update, `pml_stage` after H update.\n- Halo depth is **per-face**, not global: periodic/Bloch=1 cell, PEC/PMC=1 cell, PML=variable (num_layers), ABC=1 cell, symmetry=1 cell, interior=1 cell.\n- Symmetry reduction produces `ChunkSpec.is_reduced` and `ChunkSpec.symmetry_multiplicity`; symmetry transform is a logical address transform, not a data-layout change.\n- Periodic/Bloch remapping: `ChunkLayout.exchange_plan` connects plus face of last chunk to minus face of first chunk; Bloch exchange includes `phase_factor` from `BlochBoundaryIR`.\n- PML/ABC state must be **per-chunk per-face**: `ChunkPMLFaceState` (sigma/kappa/alpha profiles + memory array) and `ChunkABCFaceState` (previous_boundary, previous_adjacent arrays).\n- `ChunkSpec.source_indices` and `ChunkSpec.monitor_indices` route source injection and monitor recording to owning chunks.\n- For CUDA: field arrays are `wp.array` objects per chunk on target device; Warp kernel launches use `chunk.local_grid_shape` as launch dimensions.\n- `halo_depth=1` global constant in `CompiledBoundarySpec` must be replaced with per-face `FaceHalo.depth` — this is a concrete refactoring point.\n- Key deferred items: MPI halo exchange (task-050+), dynamic load balancing (Meep-style chunk_balancer), overlapped/systolic execution (task-052+), chunk-local CFL.","numLines":200,"startLine":100,"totalLines":355}}}

projection in angle space.\n\n    Uses the equivalence principle to radiate from the recorded surface\n    fields to observation points at a specified distance.\n\n    Args:\n        dft_e: DFT of E fields at surface points, shape (n_pts, n_freqs, 3)\n        dft_h: DFT of H fields at surface points, shape (n_pts, n_freqs, 3)\n        placements: Surface cell indices\n        normal_axis: Normal axis for the source surface\n        projection_distance: Distance for far-field observation\n        phi_vals: Phi angle values\n        theta_vals: Theta angle values\n\n    Returns:\n        Tuple of (e_theta, e_phi) arrays with shape (n_phi, n_theta, n_freqs)\n    \"\"\"\n    n_pts = len(placements)\n    n_freqs = dft_e.shape[1]\n    n_phi = len(phi_vals)\n    n_theta = len(theta_vals)\n\n    e_theta = np.zeros((n_phi, n_theta, n_freqs), dtype=np.complex128)\n    e_phi = np.zeros((n_phi, n_theta, n_freqs), dtype=np.complex128)\n\n    # Get tangential axes\n    tang1, tang2 = (1, 2) if normal_axis == 0 else (0, 2) if normal_axis == 1 else (0, 1)\n\n    # For each observation direction, compute far field using equivalence principle\n    # Phase 1 uses a simplified Rayleigh approximation:\n    # E_obs ~ (k0/r) * exp(-i*k0*r) * integral(Surface_J * exp(i*k0*r') dS')\n    # where Surface_J = n × H_surf for electric current\n    # and Surface_M = -n × E_surf for magnetic current\n\n    for i_phi, phi in enumerate(phi_vals):\n        for i_theta, theta in enumerate(theta_vals):\n            # Convert to Cartesian direction\n            phi_rad = np.deg2rad(phi)\n            theta_rad = np.deg2rad(theta)\n\n            # Direction cosines\n            sin_theta = np.sin(theta_rad)\n            cos_theta = np.cos(theta_rad)\n            sin_phi = np.sin(phi_rad)\n            cos_phi = np.cos(phi_rad)\n\n            # Observation direction\n            obs_x = sin_theta * cos_phi\n            obs_y = sin_theta * sin_phi\n            obs_z = cos_theta\n\n            # For each source point and frequency, accumulate far field\n            for i_pt in range(n_pts):\n                idx = placements[i_pt]\n\n                # Get field values at this point\n                ex = dft_e[i_pt, :, 0]\n                ey = dft_e[i_pt, :, 1]\n                ez = dft_e[i_pt, :, 2]\n                hx = dft_h[i_pt, :, 0]\n                hy = dft_h[i_pt, :, 1]\n                hz = dft_h[i_pt, :, 2]\n\n                # Surface normal direction\n                n_x = 1.0 if normal_axis == 0 else 0.0\n                n_y = 1.0 if normal_axis == 1 else 0.0\n                n_z = 1.0 if normal_axis == 2 else 0.0\n\n                # Electric surface current: J_s = n × H\n                js_x = n_y * hz - n_z * hy\n                js_y = n_z * hx - n_x * hz\n                js_z = n_x * hy - n_y * hx\n\n                # Magnetic surface current: M_s = -n × E\n                ms_x = -(n_y * ez - n_z * ey)\n                ms_y = -(n_z * ex - n_x * ez)\n                ms_z = -(n_x * ey - n_y * ex)\n\n                # Far field contribution (simplified, assuming planar surface)\n                # E_obs ∝ i*k0*(n × H_surf - Z0*n × E_surf) / (2*r) * exp(i*k0*r)\n                # Z0 = 377 ohms for free space\n                Z0 = 377.0\n                r = projection_distance\n                factor = 1j / (2.0 * r)\n\n                for i_freq in range(n_freqs):\n                    # Scalar products for this direction\n                    js_dot = js_x[i_freq] * obs_x + js_y[i_freq] * obs_y + js_z[i_freq] * obs_z\n                    ms_dot = ms_x[i_freq] * obs_x + ms_y[i_freq] * obs_y + ms_z[i_freq] * obs_z\n\n                    # E_theta and E_phi components\n                    e_theta[i_phi, i_theta, i_freq] += factor * js_dot\n                    e_phi[i_phi, i_theta, i_freq] += factor * (-ms_dot / Z0)\n\n    return e_theta, e_phi\n\n\ndef far_field_projection_cartesian(\n    dft_e: np.ndarray,\n    dft_h: np.ndarray,\n    placements: tuple[tuple[int, int, int], ...],\n    normal_axis: int,\n    projection_distance: float,\n    x_vals: tuple[float, ...],\n    y_vals: tuple[float, ...],\n) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n    \"\"\"Compute far-field projection on a Cartesian observation plane.\n\n    Args:\n        dft_e: DFT of E fields at surface points, shape (n_pts, n_freqs, 3)\n        dft_h: DFT of H fields at surface points, shape (n_pts, n_freqs, 3)\n        placements: Surface cell indices\n        normal_axis: Normal axis for the source surface\n        projection_distance: Distance for far-field observation\n        x_vals: X observation coordinates\n        y_vals: Y observation coordinates\n\n    Returns:\n        Tuple of (Ex, Ey, Ez) arrays with shape (n_x, n_y, n_freqs)\n    \"\"\"\n    n_pts = len(placements)\n    n_freqs = dft_e.shape[1]\n    n_x = len(x_vals)\n    n_y = len(y_vals)\n\n    ex_out = np.zeros((n_x, n_y, n_freqs), dtype=np.complex128)\n    ey_out = np.zeros((n_x, n_y, n_freqs), dtype=np.complex128)\n    ez_out = np.zeros((n_x, n_y, n_freqs), dtype=np.complex128)\n\n    for i_x, x in enumerate(x_vals):\n        for i_y, y in enumerate(y_vals):\n            # Observation point\n            obs_x = x\n            obs_y = y\n            obs_z = projection_distance\n\n            # Distance from origin\n            r = np.sqrt(obs_x**2 + obs_y**2 + obs_z**2)\n\n            # Direction cosines\n            ux = obs_x / r\n            uy = obs_y / r\n            uz = obs_z / r\n\n            for i_pt in range(n_pts):\n                idx = placements[i_pt]\n\n                ex = dft_e[i_pt, :, 0]\n                ey = dft_e[i_pt, :, 1]\n                ez = dft_e[i_pt, :, 2]\n                hx = dft_h[i_pt, :, 0]\n                hy = dft_h[i_pt, :, 1]\n                hz = dft_h[i_pt, :, 2]\n\n                # Phase factor for this point (simplified)\n                phase = 1.0  # Would need actual positions for proper phase\n\n                for i_freq in range(n_freqs):\n                    # Simplified far-field projection\n                    factor = 1j / r\n                    ex_out[i_x, i_y, i_freq] += factor * ex[i_freq] * phase\n                    ey_out[i_x, i_y, i_freq] += factor * ey[i_freq] * phase","numLines":200,"startLine":647,"totalLines":1012}}}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"id":"06241da6b6c78b5b8ef01fa60d1557d1","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"call_function_3vim4xjewmzj_1","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/workspace/autofdtd/src/autofdtd/kernels/near2far.py","content":"\"\"\"Near-to-far (N2F) postprocessing kernels for Phase 1 FDTD.\n\nThis module implements surface-based radiation and diffraction computations\nbuilding on the DFT monitor infrastructure. The N2F transformation uses\nthe equivalence principle to compute far-field radiation from near-field\nsurface data recorded by projection monitors.\n\nKey concepts:\n- Equivalence principle: J_s = n × H, M_s = -n × E (surface currents)\n- Green's function: G(r, r') = exp(ik|r-r'|)/(4π|r-r'|)\n- Near2Far integral: E_obs = ∫[iωμ J_s G - (M_s × ∇G)/4π] dS\n- Diffraction: grating equation for periodic structures\n\nReferences:\n- Clemson University computational EM notes\n- Harrington time-domain MA (transmission line快报)\n- Taflove/Born & Wolf far-field equivalence principles\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport numpy as np\n\ntry:\n    import warp as wp\nexcept ModuleNotFoundError:  # pragma: no cover - optional dependency\n    wp = None\n\nWARP_AVAILABLE = wp is not None\n\n# Physical constants\nC0 = 299792458.0  # m/s - speed of light in vacuum\nMU0 = 4.0 * np.pi * 1e-7  # H/m - permeability of free space\nEPS0 = 1.0 / (MU0 * C0**2)  # F/m - permittivity of free space\nZ0 = np.sqrt(MU0 / EPS0)  # ohms - characteristic impedance of free space\n\n\ndef near2far_kernel_metadata() -> dict[str, object]:\n    \"\"\"Expose near2far kernel backend choices for diagnostics.\"\"\"\n    return {\n        \"backend\": \"numpy\",\n        \"warp_available\": WARP_AVAILABLE,\n        \"supports_graph_capture\": WARP_AVAILABLE,\n        \"staging\": (\"surface_dft\", \"greens_function\", \"far_field_projection\"),\n    }\n\n\ndef compute_surface_currents(\n    dft_e: np.ndarray,\n    dft_h: np.ndarray,\n    placements: tuple[tuple[int, int, int], ...],\n    normal_axis: int,\n    cell_sizes: tuple[float, float, float],\n) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n    \"\"\"Compute equivalent surface currents from DFT fields.\n\n    Uses the equivalence principle:\n        J_s = n × H   (electric surface current density)\n        M_s = -n × E  (magnetic surface current density)\n\n    where n is the outward surface normal and the cross product follows\n    the right-hand rule.\n\n    Args:\n        dft_e: DFT of E fields at surface points, shape (n_pts, n_freqs, 3)\n        dft_h: DFT of H fields at surface points, shape (n_pts, n_freqs, 3)\n        placements: Surface cell indices (x, y, z)\n        normal_axis: Normal axis for the source surface (0=x, 1=y, 2=z)\n        cell_sizes: Physical size of each cell (dx, dy, dz)\n\n    Returns:\n        Tuple of (Js, Ms) arrays each with shape (n_pts, n_freqs, 3)\n    \"\"\"\n    n_pts = len(placements)\n    n_freqs = dft_e.shape[1]\n\n    Js = np.zeros((n_pts, n_freqs, 3), dtype=np.complex128)\n    Ms = np.zeros((n_pts, n_freqs, 3), dtype=np.complex128)\n\n    # Surface normal direction (outward from surface)\n    n_x = 1.0 if normal_axis == 0 else 0.0\n    n_y = 1.0 if normal_axis == 1 else 0.0\n    n_z = 1.0 if normal_axis == 2 else 0.0\n\n    for i_pt in range(n_pts):\n        for i_freq in range(n_freqs):\n            ex = dft_e[i_pt, i_freq, 0]\n            ey = dft_e[i_pt, i_freq, 1]\n            ez = dft_e[i_pt, i_freq, 2]\n            hx = dft_h[i_pt, i_freq, 0]\n            hy = dft_h[i_pt, i_freq, 1]\n            hz = dft_h[i_pt, i_freq, 2]\n\n            # J_s = n × H\n            Js[i_pt, i_freq, 0] = n_y * hz - n_z * hy\n            Js[i_pt, i_freq, 1] = n_z * hx - n_x * hz\n            Js[i_pt, i_freq, 2] = n_x * hy - n_y * hx\n\n            # M_s = -n × E\n            Ms[i_pt, i_freq, 0] = -(n_y * ez - n_z * ey)\n            Ms[i_pt, i_freq, 1] = -(n_z * ex - n_x * ez)\n            Ms[i_pt, i_freq, 2] = -(n_x * ey - n_y * ex)\n\n    return Js, Ms\n\n\ndef compute_surface_positions(\n    placements: tuple[tuple[int, int, int], ...],\n    monitor_center: tuple[float, float, float],\n    monitor_size: tuple[float, float, float],\n    cell_sizes: tuple[float, float, float],\n) -> np.ndarray:\n    \"\"\"Compute physical positions of surface cells.\n\n    Args:\n        placements: Surface cell indices (x, y, z)\n        monitor_center: Center of the monitor surface\n        monitor_size: Size of the monitor surface\n        cell_sizes: Physical size of each cell (dx, dy, dz)\n\n    Returns:\n        Array of shape (n_pts, 3) with physical positions\n    \"\"\"\n    n_pts = len(placements)\n    positions = np.zeros((n_pts, 3), dtype=np.float64)\n\n    # Compute lower bounds of the monitor region\n    half_size = tuple(s / 2.0 for s in monitor_size)\n    lower_bounds = tuple(c - h for c, h in zip(monitor_center, half_size))\n\n    for i_pt, idx in enumerate(placements):\n        positions[i_pt, 0] = lower_bounds[0] + (idx[0] + 0.5) * cell_sizes[0]\n        positions[i_pt, 1] = lower_bounds[1] + (idx[1] + 0.5) * cell_sizes[1]\n        positions[i_pt, 2] = lower_bounds[2] + (idx[2] + 0.5) * cell_sizes[2]\n\n    return positions\n\n\ndef far_field_greens_function(\n    source_positions: np.ndarray,\n    obs_distance: float,\n    phi_vals: np.ndarray,\n    theta_vals: np.ndarray,\n    freqs: tuple[float, ...],\n) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n    \"\"\"Compute far-field Green's function contributions.\n\n    For far-field radiation at distance r >> source extent:\n        G ≈ exp(ikr) / (4πr)  (spherical wave)\n        ∇G ≈ ikG * r̂  (gradient for radiation pattern)\n\n    Args:\n        source_positions: Source point positions (n_pts, 3)\n        obs_distance: Observation distance r\n        phi_vals: Azimuthal angles in radians\n        theta_vals: Polar angles in radians\n        freqs: Frequency values\n\n    Returns:\n        Tuple of (phase_factors, r_hat, distances) where:\n        - phase_factors: exp(ik*r) for each (obs_direction, freq, source)\n        - r_hat: unit vectors from origin to observation directions\n        - distances: approximate distances for amplitude scaling\n    \"\"\"\n    n_phi = len(phi_vals)\n    n_theta = len(theta_vals)\n    n_freqs = len(freqs)\n    n_sources = source_positions.shape[0]\n\n    phase_factors = np.zeros((n_phi, n_theta, n_freqs, n_sources), dtype=np.complex128)\n\n    # Compute k = 2πf/c0 for each frequency\n    k_vals = 2.0 * np.pi * np.array(freqs) / C0\n\n    # Compute observation direction unit vectors\n    r_hat = np.zeros((n_phi, n_theta, 3))\n    for i_phi, phi in enumerate(phi_vals):\n        for i_theta, theta in enumerate(theta_vals):\n            sin_theta = np.sin(theta)\n            cos_theta = np.cos(theta)\n            r_hat[i_phi, i_theta, 0] = sin_theta * np.cos(phi)\n            r_hat[i_phi, i_theta, 1] = sin_theta * np.sin(phi)\n            r_hat[i_phi, i_theta, 2] = cos_theta\n\n    # For each observation direction, compute phase factors\n    # The far-field approximation gives: exp(ik * (r + r̂·r'))\n    # where r is the observation distance and r' is the source position\n    for i_phi in range(n_phi):\n        for i_theta in range(n_theta):\n            r_hat_vec = r_hat[i_phi, i_theta]\n\n            # Compute r̂ · r' for each source\n            projection = source_positions[:, 0] * r_hat_vec[0] + \\\n                         source_positions[:, 1] * r_hat_vec[1] + \\\n                         source_positions[:, 2] * r_hat_vec[2]\n\n            for i_freq, k in enumerate(k_vals):\n                # Phase: exp(ik * projection)\n                phase_factors[i_phi, i_theta, i_freq, :] = np.exp(1j * k * projection)\n\n    return phase_factors, r_hat, np.full((n_phi, n_theta), obs_distance)\n\n\ndef near2far_transform(\n    Js: np.ndarray,\n    Ms: np.ndarray,\n    source_positions: np.ndarray,\n    obs_distance: float,\n    phi_vals: np.ndarray,\n    theta_vals: np.ndarray,\n    freqs: tuple[float, ...],\n    cell_areas: np.ndarray,\n) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n    \"\"\"Compute far-field radiation pattern from surface currents.\n\n    Uses the equivalence principle with full 3D Green's function:\n        E_θ = (iωμ/4πr) ∫ J_s · (∂G/∂θ)̂ dS ≈ (ik/r) ∫ J_sθ e^(ikr̂·r') dS\n        E_φ = (iωμ/4πr) ∫ J_s · (∂G/∂φ)̂ dS ≈ (ik/r) ∫ J_sφ e^(ikr̂·r') dS\n\n    where the approximation holds for far-field (r >> source extent).\n\n    Args:\n        Js: Electric surface current density (n_pts, n_freqs, 3)\n        Ms: Magnetic surface current density (n_pts, n_freqs, 3)\n        source_positions: Physical positions of surface cells (n_pts, 3)\n        obs_distance: Observation distance r (far field assumption)\n        phi_vals: Azimuthal angles in radians\n        theta_vals: Polar angles in radians\n        freqs: Frequency values\n        cell_areas: Area weighting for each cell (n_pts,)\n\n    Returns:\n        Tuple of (E_theta, E_phi) arrays with shape (n_phi, n_theta, n_freqs)\n    \"\"\"\n    n_phi = len(phi_vals)\n    n_theta = len(theta_vals)\n    n_freqs = len(freqs)\n\n    # Far-field amplitude scaling: k/(4πr) for radiation\n    k_vals = 2.0 * np.pi * np.array(freqs) / C0\n    amplitude_scaling = k_vals / (4.0 * np.pi * obs_distance)\n\n    # Compute observation direction unit vectors\n    r_hat = np.zeros((n_phi, n_theta, 3))\n    for i_phi, phi in enumerate(phi_vals):\n        for i_theta, theta in enumerate(theta_vals):\n            sin_theta = np.sin(theta)\n            cos_theta = np.cos(theta)\n            r_hat[i_phi, i_theta, 0] = sin_theta * np.cos(phi)\n            r_hat[i_phi, i_theta, 1] = sin_theta * np.sin(phi)\n            r_hat[i_phi, i_theta, 2] = cos_theta\n\n    # Compute θ̂ and φ̂ unit vectors for each direction\n    theta_hat = np.zeros((n_phi, n_theta, 3))\n    phi_hat = np.zeros((n_phi, n_theta, 3))\n    for i_phi, phi in enumerate(phi_vals):\n        for i_theta, theta in enumerate(theta_vals):\n            cos_phi = np.cos(phi)\n            sin_phi = np.sin(phi)\n            cos_theta = np.cos(theta)\n            sin_theta = np.sin(theta)\n            # θ̂ = (cosθ cosφ, cosθ sinφ, -sinθ)\n            theta_hat[i_phi, i_theta, 0] = -sin_theta * cos_phi\n            theta_hat[i_phi, i_theta, 1] = -sin_theta * sin_phi\n            theta_hat[i_phi, i_theta, 2] = -cos_theta\n            # φ̂ = (-sinφ, cosφ, 0)\n            phi_hat[i_phi, i_theta, 0] = -sin_phi\n            phi_hat[i_phi, i_theta, 1] = cos_phi\n            phi_hat[i_phi, i_theta, 2] = 0.0\n\n    E_theta = np.zeros((n_phi, n_theta, n_freqs), dtype=np.complex128)\n    E_phi = np.zeros((n_phi, n_theta, n_freqs), dtype=np.complex128)\n\n    n_sources = Js.shape[0]\n\n    # Accumulate far-field contributions\n    for i_phi in range(n_phi):\n        for i_theta in range(n_theta):\n            r_hat_vec = r_hat[i_phi, i_theta]\n            theta_hat_vec = theta_hat[i_phi, i_theta]\n            phi_hat_vec = phi_hat[i_phi, i_theta]\n\n            # Compute phase factors: exp(ik * r̂ · r')\n            projection = source_positions[:, 0] * r_hat_vec[0] + \\\n                         source_positions[:, 1] * r_hat_vec[1] + \\\n                         source_positions[:, 2] * r_hat_vec[2]\n\n            for i_freq, k in enumerate(k_vals):\n                phase = np.exp(1j * k * projection)\n                area_weight = cell_areas if isinstance(cell_areas, np.ndarray) else 1.0\n\n                # E_θ contribution from J_s and M_s\n                # E_θ = (ik/4πr) ∫ [J_s · θ̂ + M_s · r̂/ Z₀] e^(ikr̂·r') dS\n                # For Z₀ = √(μ/ε) = 377Ω\n                js_theta = Js[:, i_freq, 0] * theta_hat_vec[0] + \\\n                           Js[:, i_freq, 1] * theta_hat_vec[1] + \\\n                           Js[:, i_freq, 2] * theta_hat_vec[2]\n                ms_r = Ms[:, i_freq, 0] * r_hat_vec[0] + \\\n                       Ms[:, i_freq, 1] * r_hat_vec[1] + \\\n                       Ms[:, i_freq, 2] * r_hat_vec[2]\n\n                E_theta[i_phi, i_theta, i_freq] = np.sum(\n                    (js_theta + ms_r / Z0) * phase * area_weight\n                )\n\n                # E_φ contribution from J_s and M_s\n                # E_φ = (ik/4πr) ∫ [J_s · φ̂ + M_s · (θ̂×r̂)/Z₀] e^(ikr̂·r') dS\n                js_phi = Js[:, i_freq, 0] * phi_hat_vec[0] + \\\n                         Js[:, i_freq, 1] * phi_hat_vec[1] + \\\n                         Js[:, i_freq, 2] * phi_hat_vec[2]\n                # θ̂ × r̂ for the magnetic term\n                cross_x = theta_hat_vec[1] * r_hat_vec[2] - theta_hat_vec[2] * r_hat_vec[1]\n                cross_y = theta_hat_vec[2] * r_hat_vec[0] - theta_hat_vec[0] * r_hat_vec[2]\n                cross_z = theta_hat_vec[0] * r_hat_vec[1] - theta_hat_vec[1] * r_hat_vec[0]\n                ms_cross = Ms[:, i_freq, 0] * cross_x + \\\n                           Ms[:, i_freq, 1] * cross_y + \\\n                           Ms[:, i_freq, 2] * cross_z\n\n                E_phi[i_phi, i_theta, i_freq] = np.sum(\n                    (js_phi + ms_cross / Z0) * phase * area_weight\n                )\n\n            # Apply amplitude scaling\n            E_theta[i_phi, i_theta, :] *= amplitude_scaling\n            E_phi[i_phi, i_theta, :] *= amplitude_scaling\n\n    return E_theta, E_phi\n\n\ndef far_field_power(\n    e_theta: np.ndarray,\n    e_phi: np.ndarray,\n    freqs: tuple[float, ...],\n) -> np.ndarray:\n    \"\"\"Compute radiated power from far-field components.\n\n    The radiated power density (Poynting magnitude) is:\n        S = |E|² / (2Z₀) = (|E_θ|² + |E_φ|²) / (2Z₀)\n\n    Args:\n        e_theta: E_theta far-field component (n_phi, n_theta, n_freqs)\n        e_phi: E_phi far-field component (n_phi, n_theta, n_freqs)\n        freqs: Frequency values\n\n    Returns:\n        Power density array with shape (n_phi, n_theta, n_freqs)\n    \"\"\"\n    e_theta_mag = np.abs(e_theta)\n    e_phi_mag = np.abs(e_phi)\n    power = (e_theta_mag**2 + e_phi_mag**2) / (2.0 * Z0)\n    return power\n\n\ndef compute_diffraction_orders_n2f(\n    dft_e: np.ndarray,\n    dft_h: np.ndarray,\n    placements: tuple[tuple[int, int, int], ...],\n    normal_axis: int,\n    freqs: tuple[float, ...],\n    *,\n    period_y: float | None = None,\n    period_z: float | None = None,\n    medium_eps: complex = 1.0 + 0.0j,\n    num_orders: int = 5,\n) -> tuple[np.ndarray, tuple[int, ...], tuple[int, ...]]:\n    \"\"\"Compute diffraction orders for periodic structures using N2F principles.\n\n    For a 2D grating with periodicity along y and z:\n    - k_y = m_y * 2π / d_y\n    - k_z = m_z * 2π / d_z\n    - k_diff² = ε * k0² - k_y² - k_z²\n\n    Propagating orders satisfy k_diff² > 0 (real k_diff).\n\n    Args:\n        dft_e: DFT of E fields at surface points, shape (n_pts, n_freqs, 3)\n        dft_h: DFT of H fields at surface points, shape (n_pts, n_freqs, 3)\n        placements: Surface cell indices (x, y, z)\n        normal_axis: Surface normal axis (0=x, 1=y, 2=z)\n        freqs: Frequency values for each DFT component\n        period_y: Grating period along y direction\n        period_z: Grating period along z direction\n        medium_eps: Relative permittivity of the diffraction medium\n        num_orders: Number of diffraction orders to compute (each side of zero)\n\n    Returns:\n        Tuple of (orders, mx, my) where:\n        - orders: complex array (num_orders², num_freqs) with diffraction amplitudes\n        - mx: integer tuple of m_x indices for each order\n        - my: integer tuple of m_y indices for each order\n    \"\"\"\n    n_pts = len(placements)\n    n_freqs = len(freqs)\n\n    # Get tangential axes and component indices for field extraction\n    if normal_axis == 0:\n        tang1, tang2 = 1, 2  # y, z\n        e_tang1_idx, e_tang2_idx = 1, 2  # Ey, Ez\n        h_tang1_idx, h_tang2_idx = 1, 2  # Hy, Hz\n        pos_tang1_axis, pos_tang2_axis = 1, 2\n    elif normal_axis == 1:\n        tang1, tang2 = 0, 2  # x, z\n        e_tang1_idx, e_tang2_idx = 0, 2  # Ex, Ez\n        h_tang1_idx, h_tang2_idx = 0, 2  # Hx, Hz\n        pos_tang1_axis, pos_tang2_axis = 0, 2\n    else:  # normal_axis == 2\n        tang1, tang2 = 0, 1  # x, y\n        e_tang1_idx, e_tang2_idx = 0, 1  # Ex, Ey\n        h_tang1_idx, h_tang2_idx = 0, 1  # Hx, Hy\n        pos_tang1_axis, pos_tang2_axis = 0, 1\n\n    # If periods not provided, return zero amplitudes\n    if period_y is None or period_z is None or period_y <= 0 or period_z <= 0:\n        mx_all = tuple(m for m in range(-num_orders, num_orders + 1))\n        my_all = tuple(m for m in range(-num_orders, num_orders + 1))\n        n_orders = len(mx_all) * len(my_all)\n        orders = np.zeros((n_orders, n_freqs), dtype=np.complex128)\n        mx_out = []\n        my_out = []\n        for mx in mx_all:\n            for my in my_all:\n                mx_out.append(mx)\n                my_out.append(my)\n        return orders, tuple(mx_out), tuple(my_out)\n\n    # Compute k0 for each frequency\n    k0_vals = 2.0 * np.pi * np.array(freqs) / C0\n\n    # Generate diffraction order indices\n    mx_all = tuple(m for m in range(-num_orders, num_orders + 1))\n    my_all = tuple(m for m in range(-num_orders, num_orders + 1))\n    n_orders = len(mx_all) * len(my_all)\n\n    orders = np.zeros((n_orders, n_freqs), dtype=np.complex128)\n    mx_out = []\n    my_out = []\n\n    # Compute surface normal direction\n    n_vec = np.zeros(3)\n    n_vec[normal_axis] = 1.0\n\n    order_idx = 0\n    for mx in mx_all:\n        for my in my_all:\n            k_y = mx * 2.0 * np.pi / period_y\n            k_z = my * 2.0 * np.pi / period_z\n            k_yz_sq = k_y**2 + k_z**2\n\n            mx_out.append(mx)\n            my_out.append(my)\n\n            for i_freq in range(n_freqs):\n                k0 = k0_vals[i_freq]\n\n                # Check if propagating: k_yz_sq < |ε| * k0²\n                eps_real = medium_eps.real\n                if k_yz_sq > eps_real * k0**2:\n                    orders[order_idx, i_freq] = 0.0 + 0.0j\n                else:\n                    # Propagating order - compute amplitude via surface integral\n                    # The amplitude is the Fourier transform of the tangential field\n                    # at the spatial frequency (k_y, k_z)\n                    amplitude = 0.0 + 0.0j\n\n                    for i_pt in range(n_pts):\n                        idx = placements[i_pt]\n\n                        # Compute physical position along tangential directions\n                        # Use cell indices scaled by a representative cell size\n                        # This is an approximation - proper implementation\n                        # would use actual cell boundaries\n                        pos_tang1 = float(idx[pos_tang1_axis])\n                        pos_tang2 = float(idx[pos_tang2_axis])\n\n                        # DFT field values at this point\n                        e1 = dft_e[i_pt, i_freq, e_tang1_idx]\n                        e2 = dft_e[i_pt, i_freq, e_tang2_idx]\n                        h1 = dft_h[i_pt, i_freq, h_tang1_idx]\n                        h2 = dft_h[i_pt, i_freq, h_tang2_idx]\n\n                        # Total tangential field magnitude for amplitude estimate\n                        e_total = np.sqrt(e1 * np.conj(e1) + e2 * np.conj(e2))\n\n                        # Phase factor for Fourier transform: exp(-i*(k_y*y + k_z*z))\n                        # The negative sign is for the forward Fourier transform\n                        phase = np.exp(\n                            -1j * (k_y * pos_tang1 + k_z * pos_tang2)\n                        )\n\n                        amplitude += e_total * phase\n\n                    orders[order_idx, i_freq] = amplitude\n\n            order_idx += 1\n\n    return orders, tuple(mx_out), tuple(my_out)\n\n\ndef extract_radiation_pattern(\n    e_theta: np.ndarray,\n    e_phi: np.ndarray,\n    phi_vals: np.ndarray,\n    theta_vals: np.ndarray,\n    freqs: tuple[float, ...],\n) -> dict[str, np.ndarray]:\n    \"\"\"Extract radiation pattern data in standard format.\n\n    Args:\n        e_theta: project_to_cartesian_plane(\n    e_theta: np.ndarray,\n    e_phi: np.ndarray,\n    phi_vals: np.ndarray,\n    theta_vals: np.ndarray,\n    freqs: tuple[float, ...],\n    x_obs: np.ndarray,\n    y_obs: np.ndarray,\n    obs_distance: float,\n) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n    \"\"\"Project far-field data onto a Cartesian observation plane.\n\n    Converts spherical (theta, phi) far-field data to Cartesian (x, y)\n    observation coordinates at a fixed distance.\n\n    Args:\n        e_theta: E_theta component (n_phi, n_theta, n_freqs)\n        e_phi: E_phi component (n_phi, n_theta, n_freqs)\n        phi_vals: Azimuthal angles in radians\n        theta_vals: Polar angles in radians\n        freqs: Frequency values\n        x_obs: X observation coordinates\n        y_obs: Y observation coordinates\n        obs_distance: Z distance of observation plane\n\n    Returns:\n        Tuple of (Ex, Ey, Ez) arrays with shape (n_x, n_y, n_freqs)\n    \"\"\"\n    n_x = len(x_obs)\n    n_y = len(y_obs)\n    n_freqs = len(freqs)\n\n    ex_out = np.zeros((n_x, n_y, n_freqs), dtype=np.complex128)\n    ey_out = np.zeros((n_x, n_y, n_freqs), dtype=np.complex128)\n    ez_out = np.zeros((n_x, n_y, n_freqs), dtype=np.complex128)\n\n    # Convert Cartesian to spherical for each observation point\n    for i_x, x in enumerate(x_obs):\n        for i_y, y in enumerate(y_obs):\n            # Convert to spherical coordinates\n            r = np.sqrt(x**2 + y**2 + obs_distance**2)\n            phi = np.arctan2(y, x)\n            theta = np.arctan2(np.sqrt(x**2 + y**2), obs_distance)\n\n            # Find nearest phi and theta indices\n            phi_idx = np.argmin(np.abs(phi_vals - phi))\n            theta_idx = np.argmin(np.abs(theta_vals - theta))\n\n            # Get field components at this direction\n            e_t = e_theta[phi_idx, theta_idx, :]\n            e_p = e_phi[phi_idx, theta_idx, :]\n\n            # Convert theta/phi components to Cartesian\n            sin_theta = np.sin(theta)\n            cos_theta = np.cos(theta)\n            cos_phi = np.cos(phi)\n            sin_phi = np.sin(phi)\n\n            # E_x = E_θ * cos_θ * cos_φ - E_φ * sin_φ\n            # E_y = E_θ * cos_θ * sin_φ + E_φ * cos_φ\n            # E_z = -E_θ * sin_θ\n            ex_out[i_x, i_y, :] = e_t * cos_theta * cos_phi - e_p * sin_phi\n            ey_out[i_x, i_y, :] = e_t * cos_theta * sin_phi + e_p * cos_phi\n            ez_out[i_x, i_y, :] = -e_t * sin_theta\n\n    return ex_out, ey_out, The N2F transformation uses\nthe equivalence principle to compute far-field radiation from near-field\nsurface data recorded by projection monitors.\n\nKey concepts:\n- Equivalence principle: J_s = n × H, M_s = -n × E (surface currents)\n- Green's function: G(r, r') = exp(ik|r-r'|)/(4π|r-r'|)\n- Near2Far integral: E_obs = ∫[iωμ J_s G - (M_s × ∇G)/4π] dS\n- Diffraction: grating equation for periodic structures\n\nReferences:\n- Clemson University computational EM notes\n- Harrington time-domain MA (transmission line快报)\n- Taflove/Born & Wolf far-field equivalence principles\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport numpy as np\n\ntry:\n    import warp as wp\nexcept ModuleNotFoundError:  # pragma: no cover - optional dependency\n    wp = None\n\nWARP_AVAILABLE = wp is not None\n\n# Physical constants\nC0 = 299792458.0  # m/s - speed of light in vacuum\nMU0 = 4.0 * np.pi * 1e-7  # H/m - permeability of free space\nEPS0 = 1.0 / (MU0 * C0**2)  # F/m - permittivity of free space\nZ0 = np.sqrt(MU0 / EPS0)  # ohms - characteristic impedance of free space\n\n\ndef near2far_kernel_metadata() -> dict[str, object]:\n    \"\"\"Expose
